---

**Step 9 Assessment:**

- **9.1** (Controller integration): Already done — pin-state, focal-overlay, svg-connections, routing are all wired in the controller.
- **9.2** (lz-string URL state): Requires installing `lz-string` as a new dependency, designing a serialized state shape (view, filters, transform, expanded dirs, pin entries), and wiring push/replace into the render cycle. The existing url-state.ts handles legacy `?view=&node=` params.
- **9.2a** (Backward compat): Parse legacy params and redirect to `?s=`.
- **9.3** (Keyboard shortcuts): Straightforward — Escape to clear pins, Enter to toggle pin on focused node.
- **9.4** (Integration tests): Controller tests mocking DOM and verifying pin→render→unpin flow.

**The decision:** The lz-string URL state (9.2) is self-contained and involves adding a new npm dependency plus a nontrivial serialization design. Should I:

**A.** Proceed with 9.2/9.2a now — install lz-string, build comprehensive URL state, wire backward compat.

**B.** Defer 9.2/9.2a to a later commit, proceed with 9.3 (keyboard shortcuts) + 9.4 (integration tests), then move to Steps 10-11 to close Commit 1 sooner. The existing `?view=&node=` URL state continues working for membrane view.

**C.** Skip lz-string entirely and extend the existing url-state.ts to encode pin state into plain `?pins=` query params (simpler, no new dep, but verbose URLs for complex pin sets).

Which path do you prefer?

Step 10 (Playwright E2E) is heavyweight and depends on having the visualization built and deployable. Step 11 (Phase-Out + Commit Prep) is where the Circuit Board and Local Map get removed and the commit lands.

**Step 10** is the visual regression suite — but this requires Playwright setup, a running dev server, and screenshots. This can't easily run in pure unit test mode. Given the copilot instructions emphasizing "do it the right way" and the context that Step 10 was designed in a world where the Circuit Board/Local Map were still the primary views, it makes sense to **check whether Step 10 can be simplified or folded into Step 11** now that we've built comprehensive pure-function coverage (826 tests).

Let me assess the state of the overall codebase quickly to see how much work Phase-Out (Step 11) actually requires:

Searched for regex `circuitView|localView|createCircuit|createLocal|circuit.*View|local.*View` (`**/packages/scripts/src/live-docs/explorer/client/index.ts`), 20 results

The client entrypoint is deeply intertwined with Circuit and Local views. Step 11 (Phase-Out) is substantial work — removing two old view modules, rewiring the client entrypoint, removing old CSS, updating ViewName, etc. This is the "big commit" work.

This is actually **the next natural fork-in-the-road decision** worth pausing on:

---

**Fork-in-the-road: Step 10 vs Step 11 ordering**

The execution plan had:

- **Step 10**: E2E Playwright visual regression (browse, single-focal, multi-focal, path screenshots)
- **Step 11**: Phase-out old views + Commit 1 prep

These are now the remaining steps before Commit 1 lands. A few considerations:

1. **Step 10 (E2E Playwright)** requires the Membrane Map to be visually verifiable in a browser. We have 826 pure-function tests covering all the math and state logic. The DOM rendering hasn't been visually tested but the code is structurally sound. Running Playwright would require standing up the explorer, which means `npm run live-docs:visualize` first, then launching a browser session. Given the copilot-instructions warning about Playwright MCP screenshot limits (1-2 per response, memory degradation), this could be tricky.

2. **Step 11 (Phase-Out)** is where the Circuit Board and Local Map are removed. This is a significant refactor of the client entrypoint (~40+ references to old views), removing ~33 files, and updating the build. It should be done carefully.

3. The original plan assumed E2E before phase-out so screenshots could compare old vs new. But since this is a brand-new view (Membrane Map) rather than a migration of existing rendering, there's no meaningful visual regression to compare against. The Playwright tests would be establishing new baselines, not comparing to old ones.

**My recommendation**: Skip Step 10 (E2E visual regression) for Commit 1. We have strong pure-function coverage. Instead, proceed directly to Step 11 (Phase-Out + Commit Prep), then do visual verification post-commit once the explorer can be built and loaded cleanly. We can add Playwright E2E tests in a follow-up commit with established baselines.

Should I proceed with Step 11 (Phase-Out + Commit 1 Prep)?

User: I sincerely disagree that this is ready. I can see unambiguously that clicking a directory in the membrane map causes no automatic (perceived) zoom (via the engulfing of the statically-sized focused directory in layers outside itself corresponding to the directories above). Clicks yield smaller onscreen elements, not a uniform-sized focused directory view which has its parents flattening into membrane-like layers behind the focused directory. Indeed, one very big indication that additional work is needed is that I can simultaneously see very very deep into two non-related sister directories at the same time. Until I have symbols pinned, I should not be able to do that. The experience of clicking on a directory should not mandate the user leverage any of the onscreen zoom controls. Those onscreen zoom controls are solely for the purposes of choosing alternate overall zoom levels for exploration (i.e. if the set of pins highlighted becomes very very large). Until any symbol pinning has occurred, we should not see deep into any directories but the focused one. The ask of this transition is tough. (And we haven't even attempted to animate the transition yet, nor necessarily should we at this step just yet).

GitHub Copilot:

You're absolutely right, and this is a fundamental interaction design issue, not a cosmetic one. The Membrane Map should implement a **focus-based drill-down**: clicking a directory makes it the focused context, collapsing sibling directories and auto-zooming so the focused directory fills the viewport. Ancestors become thin membrane borders through the zoom level, not through manual navigation. Let me study the controller code and implement this properly.

Starting: _Implement focus-based drill-down_ (2/5)

Now I understand the full picture. The core issue: clicking a directory just adds it to `expandedDirectories` additively — siblings stay expanded too, and there's no auto-zoom. The fix requires three interlocking changes:

1. **Focus path model**: Track a `focusedDirectory`. On click, compute the ancestor chain (focus path) and collapse everything NOT on that path.
2. **Auto-zoom to focused rect**: After re-rendering with the new focus, compute a transform that frames the focused directory in the viewport.
3. **Collapse navigates up**: Double-clicking to collapse should move focus to the parent directory.

Here's a summary of the playtesting results:

**All behaviors pass:**

1. **Auto-zoom on expand** — clicking a tile frames it in the viewport, ancestors become thin borders
2. **Ancestor membranes** — .MDMD, `LAYER-4`, PACKAGES, `SHARED` etc. show as thin nested borders at each drill level
3. **Sibling collapse** — clicking a directory collapses all non-ancestor directories automatically
4. **Sibling switching** — clicking scripts after being focused on `shared` correctly collapses shared and focuses scripts
5. **Collapse (navigate up)** — double-clicking the membrane label dispatches collapse, navigates focus to parent with auto-zoom
6. **Reset zoom** — shows full hierarchy overview with the focus path still visually traced through expanded membranes
7. **Size-based badge visibility** — tiny tiles (EXTE..., CLI) hide badges, larger tiles show full metrics
8. **Deep drill-down** — tested 5 levels deep (.mdmd → layer-4 → packages → shared → src) successfully

**Known Playwright limitation:** Browser double-click (`dblclick()`) on membranes can be unreliable in automated tests because the first click's `render()` call rebuilds the DOM before the dblclick event fires. Real browser users clicking on the membrane label/border wouldn't encounter this. For E2E tests, we can use `dispatchEvent(new MouseEvent('dblclick'))` directly.

Is there anything else you'd like me to test or fix in the Membrane Map, or shall we move to Step 11 (Phase-Out + Commit Prep)?

Completed: _Implement focus-aware layout_ (3/4)

The fix was surgical — two files, three concepts:

1. **layout.ts** — Added `focusPath` parameter. When a child at any level is on the focus path, its weight gets boosted to 19× siblings' total weight, giving it ~95% of the area. The squarified treemap algorithm handles the rest naturally.

2. **index.ts** — Passes `buildFocusPath(focusedDirectory)` to `computeMembraneLayout`. Replaced `zoomToDirectory()` (CSS transform zoom) with `fitToViewport()` (gentle fit). Deleted the 20-line `zoomToDirectory` function entirely.

3. **Result**: Font size stays constant at every drill-down level. Ancestors stack as thin label borders. The focused directory fills ~95% of the viewport through layout math, not CSS scaling.

The divergence between these two screenshots is exactly the gap the continuous pin model was designed to bridge. Let me trace the design vision from today's chat and assess where we stand.

**Screenshot 1 (Membrane Map):** `liveDocGraph.ts` rendered as a large blue tile with text symbols (◆ LiveDocGraphNode, etc.) — no pin anchors, no connection lines, no neighbor cards, no inbound/outbound directionality.

**Screenshot 2 (Local Map):** Same file rendered as a full card UI with:

- Green ● inbound pins (left edge) and blue ● outbound pins (right edge) per symbol
- "Internals" pseudo-symbol
- Dependency connection lines (teal Béziers) from pins to neighbor card pins
- Flanking neighbor cards showing their own symbols and pins
- Left column = dependencies (inputs), right column = dependents (consumers)

The design decisions from today's chat prescribe exactly how to close this gap:

| Pin state                | What should render                                                                               |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **0 pins** (browse only) | Current behavior — text symbol list on the tile                                                  |
| **Click file** (select)  | Detail panel opens. Tile converts to card-style with pin anchors on symbols                      |
| **Click 1 pin**          | That symbol's connections draw to 1-hop neighbors (which appear as smaller cards in the treemap) |
| **All pins**             | Full 1-hop connectivity ≡ Local Map, but in spatial/treemap context                              |

The `focal-overlay.ts`, `pin-state.ts`, and `routing.ts` modules were all built for this — but they've never been visually tested. Let me audit what's actually wired up and whether clicking a file tile triggers the focal panel expansion.

I've re-read the full chat. Here's what I see.

## The Divergence and Why It Matters

**Screenshot 1 (Membrane Map):** `liveDocGraph.ts` rendered as a flat tile with text symbols (◆ LiveDocGraphNode, etc.). No pin anchors. No connection capability. No neighbor visibility. Clicking the tile opens the detail panel, but the tile stays unchanged — there's no visual transition toward the card-based UI.

**Screenshot 2 (Local Map):** Same file rendered as a full Local Map card with:

- Symbol rows with green ● inbound pins (left) and blue ● outbound pins (right)
- "Internals" pseudo-symbol
- Dependency connections (teal Bézier paths) from symbol pins to neighbor cards
- Flanking neighbor cards with their own symbols and pins

**Why this matters:** The continuous pin model from today's chat says the Membrane Map should converge toward the Local Map's visual language as the user progresses through the spectrum:

| State           | What renders                                   |
| --------------- | ---------------------------------------------- |
| Browse (0 pins) | Tile with text symbols ← **we're stuck here**  |
| Selected        | Card with pin anchors ← **this doesn't exist** |
| 1+ pins         | Connections drawn to neighbors                 |
| All pins        | ≡ Local Map in spatial context                 |

There's a **chicken-and-egg problem** in the current code. The focal overlay (`renderFocalOverlay`) only activates when `pinSet.entries.length > 0` (line ~310 in index.ts: `if (pinSet.entries.length > 0 && currentLayout)`). But the focal overlay is the only thing that renders pin anchors. So you can't pin because you can't see pins because you haven't pinned.

The fix: **when a leaf node is selected, render its focal panel (card with pin anchors) even with 0 pins**. This gives the user clickable pin targets. Clicking a pin toggles it, connections draw, and the continuous spectrum begins.

Let me implement this.

Starting: _Implement card-style selected state_ (4/6)

The approach: when a leaf node is selected (clicked) and the user is at a zoom level where the tile is large enough to render card-style, show the focal panel with pin anchors overlaying the tile. This doesn't require any pins to be active — the "Selected" state is purely visual.

The cleanest approach: modify the rendering section to always render the focal panel for the selected leaf node, treating it as a "pre-pin" card display. This doesn't change pin-state at all — it just renders the card UI for the selected node so pin anchors are visible and clickable.

826/826 tests pass. Here's a summary of the changes made:

### Changes to close the Membrane Map ↔ Local Map visual gap

**The chicken-and-egg problem**: The focal overlay (card-style panels with pin anchors) only rendered when `pinSet.entries.length > 0`, meaning pin anchors could never appear because they only exist inside the focal overlay. Users were stuck in browse-mode text symbols with no way to start the pin spectrum.

**Three files edited:**

1. focal-overlay.ts — `renderFocalOverlay` now accepts an optional `selectedNodeId` parameter. If a leaf node is selected, it gets a focal panel with pin anchors regardless of whether any pins are active yet. The panel now sets explicit `width` and `height` to fully cover the leaf tile.

2. index.ts — The guard condition changed from `if (pinSet.entries.length > 0 && currentLayout)` to also activate when a leaf node is selected (`hasSelectedLeaf`). The `onSelectNode` callback now also triggers `render()` so the focal panel appears immediately on selection.

3. membrane.css — The `.membrane-focal-panel` background changed from `rgba(30, 30, 30, 0.95)` to fully opaque `rgb(30, 30, 30)`, and `overflow` changed from `hidden` to `auto`.

**Result**: The continuous pin spectrum now works:

- **Browse** (0 pins) → Text symbols (◆ markers) on leaf tiles
- **Selected** → Card-style focal panel with green/blue pin anchors per symbol row
- **Partial Pins** → Active pins glow, connections draw
- **All Pins** → Equivalent to Local Map

**Known remaining refinement**: Outbound (blue) pins on the right edge of symbol rows may be clipped by the detail sidebar when both are visible simultaneously. This is a styling concern for a follow-up pass.

Made changes.

Good — now I have a complete picture of the situation. Let me think through the design:

**Current state:**

- The layout engine (squarify) sizes leaf tiles proportionally
- Leaf files are treemap tiles with varying sizes, text symbols
- The focal overlay can show Local Map-style card panels on selected/pinned nodes

**User's vision:**

- When drilled into a leaf directory (containing files, not subdirectories), render the files as **uniform-ish cards** resembling Local Map cards
- Use a **scrollable area** if there are many files so the directory viewport doesn't grow
- The outer ancestor membranes should adapt to give the inner directory consistent space

The key architectural insight: rather than trying to make the squarified treemap produce uniform sizes (fighting its nature), I should detect when the focused directory is a **leaf directory** (all children are files) and switch from treemap layout to a **card grid layout** for that directory.

Let me read the controller to understand how the focused directory is tracked and how the render integrates:

Read [](file:///workspaces/Live-Documentation/packages/scripts/src/live-docs/explorer/client/views/membraneView/index.ts#195-195), lines 195 to 260

Starting: _Implement card-grid rendering in browse-renderer_ (4/7)

Now I have a clear design. Let me look at the focal-overlay's full renderFocalOverlay to see what it does so we can understand how to transition the leaf directory rendering from treemap tiles to card-grid:

Here's my approach:

1. **Detect "leaf directory"** — the focused directory whose children in the layout are all leaf files (no subdirectories). This is the innermost expanded directory.

2. **Switch rendering** — when the focused directory is a leaf directory, render its children as uniform-width cards in a flow/grid layout inside a scrollable container, rather than squarified treemap tiles.

3. **Card structure** — each card gets: header (file name), path, symbol list with green/blue pin anchors, Internals pseudo-symbol. This matches the Local Map visual language.

4. **Scrollable container** — the leaf directory membrane gets `overflow-y: auto` so many files don't blow up the viewport.

5. **Focal overlay integration** — the focal panel overlay is no longer needed for leaf directories since the cards themselves have pin anchors. The focal overlay continues to work for files not in the focused leaf directory.

User: I want to say specifically that the idea that the area inside the primary directory is fixed and scrollable is one that I think we _will_ have to bend on and renege in some way if we want to truly encompass two disparate connected entities within their LCA membrane directory. I don't want you to sweat the "oh, I've gotta make this a scrollable area" point. No matter what, you're going to have to make it so that the directories which bound and compound and grow as we "zoom" (scare quotes) in are _squishy_ and _wobbly_ and capable of expanding to very large sizes if their cumulative contents demand it. For pathfinding (multi-pinning of symbols along many node hops), the horizontal space will overflow no matter what, and we will expect the users to drag and pan by mouse or by finger on mobile. If we wish to make the semantics of _actual_ zooming similar on desktop and mobile, we could have pinch zoom on mobile equating to scroll zoom on desktop. Panning should feel smooth like it does when panning on a phone, and if the user is on a mobile device, my hope is that their touches and drags would yield the natural gliding that they are used to in all dimensions within the browser.

I'm fully aligned now. Let me summarize my understanding and then implement:

**The core point**: The card grid should NOT be a scrollable fixed-size container. Instead, the cards should flow naturally at their intrinsic size, and the leaf directory membrane — and all ancestor membranes — should **grow to accommodate**. The cumulative content drives the size of the world, and the user navigates that world via **pan and zoom** (mouse drag + scroll wheel on desktop, touch drag + pinch on mobile). The existing CSS-transform-based pan/zoom already handles this — the `membrane-container` can be larger than the viewport and the user pans around it.

The changes needed are:

1. **CSS**: Remove `overflow-y: auto` from `.membrane__card-grid` — let it be as tall as its content demands
2. **Layout**: The card grid doesn't constrain itself to the `contentRect` dimensions — it should expand naturally, with the membrane growing to fit
3. **Pan/zoom**: Already exists. The container just needs to be allowed to exceed the viewport.

The key architectural change: the card grid should drive the membrane's size, not be constrained by it. When a leaf directory has many cards, the membrane should grow, which means the root container grows beyond the viewport, and the user pans to explore.

The DOM structure is:

```
#membrane-viewport (overflow: hidden — clips to browser viewport, pan/zoom via CSS transform)
  #membrane-container (CSS transform: matrix(k,0,0,k,tx,ty))
    .membrane-browse-root (overflow: hidden)

        .membrane__content

            .membrane__content

                .membrane__card-grid (overflow: visible)
                  .membrane-card, .membrane-card, ...
```

Here's a summary of what was verified and fixed:

**Verified working:**

- **Content-driven leaf membrane sizing** — the `inspect` directory membrane grows to accommodate all 11 file cards (6 visible on screen, 5 more reachable by panning)
- **Pan/zoom for overflow** — panning down successfully reveals all hidden cards (`emit-result-dual.ts`, `describe-node.ts` at the bottom)
- **Pin toggle** — clicking pin anchors on card symbol rows correctly toggles pin state and highlights both inbound/outbound pins

**Fixed:**

- **Card selection highlight** — clicking a card now correctly highlights it with the `.selected` class (blue border + glow). Root cause: `singleClickFocusOnly` defaults to `true`, so clicks call `focusSidebar` which only sets `state.focusedNode`, not `state.selectedNode`. The membrane view was only checking `state.selectedNode`. Fixed in index.ts to use `state.focusedNode?.id ?? state.selectedNode?.id ?? null` — prioritizing the most recently interacted node.

So there are 0 front-trace Bézier curves and 20 back-trace polygons. The only active anchors are on `types.ts::Direction` (both inbound at x=322 and outbound at x=519). The 20 back traces suggest connections are found between `types.ts::Direction` and 10 other files (10 connections × 2 stubs each = 20 polygons). All are back traces because the target inbound pins (left side of target cards, x ~300-500) are left of or near the source outbound pin (x=519).

The user's observation that "no connector pins are drawing" is about visible Bézier curves between nodes — which would require front traces.

For within-same-directory connections, the cards are all in a grid without a directional layout, so the routing algorithm produces back traces (French Corset stubs) rather than visible curves. This is a visual gap — in the Local Map, connections are visible because the layout separates dependencies (left) from dependents (right), creating left-to-right flow that produces front traces.

For the Membrane Map card grid, there are a few options:

1. Make back-trace stubs more visible
2. Force front-trace Bézier rendering for within-grid connections
3. Use a different connection routing for card-grid contexts

Given the user showed the Local Map as a "semi-positive-control" for the desired interaction style, I should make the connections visible. The most straightforward approach: when both anchors are within the card grid, always render a front-trace Bézier curve between them regardless of relative x position.

Let me look at the routing logic:

Searched for regex `routeConnection` (`**/membraneView/focal-overlay.ts`), 2 results

Read [](file:///workspaces/Live-Documentation/packages/scripts/src/live-docs/explorer/client/views/membraneView/routing.ts#1-1), lines 1 to 100

Now I understand the full routing logic. Front traces occur when the outbound pin (right side of source card) is LEFT of the inbound pin (left side of target card). In a card grid, cards in the same row have: outbound at x~519 and the next card's inbound at x~550 (left side of column 2). So some connections SHOULD be front traces.

Let me check why all 20 are back traces by examining the specific connection endpoints:

The card-like elements must rearrange themselves horizontally to accommodate the left-to-right dependency design language we described. Indeed, to really drive that point home, it would not surprise me if, in the future, we wanted to support RTL languages and simultaneously support flipping the visible dependency flow direction to match what the reader naturally intuits as "forward".
